# Attestation queue — licensed-source dose claims

Every row is a **dose, strength, interval or course duration** paraphrased from a
**licensed** source. **No quote is stored**, so `verify.py` cannot check these: they
must be read against a current subscription and attested by a person.

> ### This file IS the record. Tick it in place.
> | you write | means |
> |---|---|
> | *(empty)* | not yet attested |
> | `KL 2026-09-23` | **CONFIRMED** against the subscription |
> | `! KL 2026-09-23 AMH says 400 mg` | ⚠️ **REJECTED** — the claim is wrong. **`verify.py` FAILS until the guideline is fixed.** |
> | `~ owner 2026-09-24` | **OWNER-ACCEPTED, not individually checked** against the subscription. Counted separately; never reported as confirmed. Overwrite with initials and date once checked. |
>
> Regenerating this file preserves your ticks. ⚠️ **A tick is matched on `ref` AND `sha`**
> — if the claim is reworded the sha changes, the tick is dropped, and the row returns to
> the queue. **No sign-off is ever carried onto text nobody read.**

| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | chronic-pain#10 | `50c3f4f7` |  | If there is no benefit from an opioid trial, TAPER THE DOSE BEFORE STOPPING because of the risk of withdrawal symptoms; if continuing beyond the trial REVIEW WEEKLY, and TAPER AND STOP if no improvement is seen with escalating doses such as more than 50 mg morphine equivalent daily, or if adverse effects become unacceptable |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | chronic-pain#11 | `af4c7592` |  | AMH states that opioid TREATMENT SHOULD NOT LAST MORE THAN 90 DAYS, FOLLOWED BY PLANNED TAPERING |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | chronic-pain#12 | `271ebd6d` |  | For NEUROPATHIC pain, consider non-drug treatment such as exercise and use drugs as part of an integrated multidisciplinary strategy; AGREE ON CRITERIA for success or failure, ensure the patient understands improvement may not occur, START AT A LOW DOSE and gradually titrate to the lowest effective dose, and ASSESS BENEFIT AFTER 4 TO 8 WEEKS, ceasing or switching if there is no benefit or unacceptable adverse effects |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | chronic-pain#13 | `100b520f` |  | TCAs such as amitriptyline, ANTISEIZURE DRUGS such as gabapentin and pregabalin, and SNRIs such as duloxetine are the DRUG TREATMENTS OF CHOICE for neuropathic pain -- HOWEVER EFFICACY STUDIES ARE SHORT-TERM, UP TO 16 WEEKS, AND PROVIDE NO EVIDENCE OF CLINICAL SUPERIORITY FOR AN INDIVIDUAL DRUG; comparative studies are limited and long-term data are lacking |

| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | contraception#37 | `bff48f59` |  | AMH gives depot medroxyprogesterone as an intramuscular injection every 12 weeks with a failure rate of 0.2 to 6%, providing prolonged contraception whose efficacy is unaffected by drug interactions, but which cannot be removed, may delay return to fertility after stopping rarely beyond 12 months, and may cause amenorrhoea, irregular bleeding, breast tenderness, acne, weight gain and decreased bone mineral density |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | contraception#38 | `30e53990` |  | AMH gives the etonogestrel implant a failure rate of 0.05% providing long-term contraception for 3 years, inserted and removed by a doctor, with efficacy affected by some drugs and possible amenorrhoea, irregular bleeding, acne and breast tenderness |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | contraception#39 | `0f5cb52c` |  | AMH gives the levonorgestrel IUD a failure rate of 0.2% providing long-term contraception for 5 or 8 years with lighter, less painful periods and efficacy unaffected by drug interactions, inserted and removed by a doctor, and notes it increases the risk of pelvic infection for 3 weeks after insertion and the device may be expelled especially in the first year |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | contraception#40 | `9e55178b` |  | AMH gives the copper IUD a failure rate of 0.6 to 0.8% providing long-term contraception for 5 or 10 years with efficacy unaffected by drug interactions, but which may cause heavy periods and period pain, increases the risk of pelvic infection for 3 weeks after insertion, and may be expelled especially in the first year |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | contraception#41 | `aac13bec` |  | AMH gives barrier method failure rates as 2 to 18% for the male condom, 5 to 21% for the female condom and 6 to 12% for the diaphragm |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | contraception#47 | `debaa107` |  | AMH states that contraception is not necessary in the first 21 days after delivery |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | contraception#48 | `9094372b` |  | After delivery condoms can be used immediately and a diaphragm can be fitted and used from 6 weeks postpartum when vaginal tone returns |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | contraception#49 | `9a79e9ab` |  | Progestogen-only contraceptives may be used at any time after delivery except the levonorgestrel IUD, and if depot medroxyprogesterone is used before 6 weeks postpartum it may cause heavy irregular bleeding |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | contraception#50 | `ce5e55a9` |  | Use of COCs and the combined hormonal vaginal ring should be delayed until at least 21 days postpartum as there may be an increased risk of thrombosis before this time |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | contraception#51 | `a7e1c0eb` |  | An IUD including the levonorgestrel IUD may be inserted either within 48 hours of delivery or more than 4 weeks after delivery, usually more than 6 weeks after delivery |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | contraception#55 | `30a6fc6d` |  | Contraception should generally be continued for 1 year after the last period if over 50 years, and for 2 years if under 50, and menopausal hormone therapy is not contraceptive |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | contraception#57 | `05a6c227` |  | If a copper IUD is inserted at age 40 or more it may be used until menopause without being replaced, and if the 52 mg levonorgestrel IUD is inserted at age 45 or more it may be used until age 55, or if amenorrhoeic until menopause occurs, without being replaced |

| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | epilepsy#7 | `0a302c62` |  | Treatment is usually indicated when 2 OR MORE SEIZURES HAVE OCCURRED WITHIN 6 TO 12 MONTHS -- ABOUT 80% OF PEOPLE WILL HAVE RECURRENT SEIZURES AFTER 2 SEIZURES -- except with a clear avoidable precipitant or with some seizure types such as benign childhood epilepsy with centrotemporal spikes |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | epilepsy#19 | `0a308b5b` |  | VIGABATRIN SHOULD BE USED ONLY WHEN ALL OTHER APPROPRIATE DRUGS HAVE FAILED: IT MAY CAUSE IRREVERSIBLE VISUAL FIELD DEFECTS IN 20 TO 40% OF PATIENTS, and because visual fields are difficult to monitor in young children it should be considered for infantile spasms only where there are no safer alternatives |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | epilepsy#21 | `d830eb39` |  | START TREATMENT WITH ONE FIRST-LINE DRUG ONLY and INCREASE THE DOSE GRADUALLY, ESPECIALLY FOR CARBAMAZEPINE AND LAMOTRIGINE; ABOUT HALF OF ALL PATIENTS ARE SYMPTOM FREE, with manageable adverse effects, ON THE FIRST ANTISEIZURE DRUG TRIED |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | epilepsy#22 | `220f6d4b` |  | BEFORE CHANGING DRUG TREATMENT CHECK THREE THINGS: COMPLIANCE -- noncompliance is a common reason for treatment failure and plasma concentration monitoring can help; DOSE -- ensure the first-line drug is at maximal dose with minimal adverse effects; and DIAGNOSIS -- re-evaluate and confirm it |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | epilepsy#23 | `77148fda` |  | If treatment is still ineffective, ADD an alternative first-line or a second-line drug, and WHEN THE OPTIMAL DOSAGE OF THE SECOND DRUG HAS BEEN ACHIEVED, GRADUALLY DECREASE the dosage of the first |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | epilepsy#25 | `450b7464` |  | IN PEOPLE WITH UNCONTROLLED SEIZURES TAKING MORE THAN ONE ANTISEIZURE DRUG, SIMPLIFYING THE REGIMEN MAY IMPROVE SEIZURE CONTROL: AN EFFECTIVE DOSE OF ONE DRUG IS BETTER THAN INEFFECTIVE DOSES OF TWO OR MORE |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | epilepsy#26 | `0efdb083` |  | Treatment withdrawal may be attempted AFTER 2 TO 3 YEARS WITHOUT SEIZURES; good prognostic signs are a history of FEW seizures, ABSENCE SEIZURES ONLY, YOUNGER AGE when control was achieved, NORMAL EEG, NORMAL NEUROLOGICAL EXAMINATION and ABSENCE OF BRAIN LESIONS |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | epilepsy#27 | `7ccfc75c` |  | DO NOT STOP ANTISEIZURE TREATMENT ABRUPTLY -- INCREASED SEIZURES AND STATUS EPILEPTICUS MAY OCCUR; reduce the dosage OVER SEVERAL WEEKS TO MONTHS, UP TO 6 MONTHS OR LONGER FOR BENZODIAZEPINES OR BARBITURATES, and WITHDRAW ONE DRUG AT A TIME |

| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | epilepsy#38 | `7c585569` |  | THE INCIDENCE OF CONGENITAL MALFORMATIONS IN INFANTS OF MOTHERS TREATED WITH ANTISEIZURE DRUGS IS APPROXIMATELY 2 TO 3 TIMES HIGHER THAN IN THE GENERAL POPULATION -- 4 TO 6% VERSUS 2 TO 3%. THE RISK APPEARS HIGHEST WITH VALPROATE AT APPROXIMATELY 10% AND LOWEST WITH LAMOTRIGINE AND LEVETIRACETAM AT APPROXIMATELY 3% |

| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | epilepsy#41 | `fd7ce275` |  | HEPATIC ENZYME INDUCERS -- carbamazepine, phenytoin, barbiturates, oxcarbazepine, topiramate -- MAY CAUSE VITAMIN K DEFICIENCY IN THE FETUS, increasing the risk of neonatal bleeding: ENSURE IM VITAMIN K 1 mg IS GIVEN TO THE BABY IMMEDIATELY AFTER BIRTH; antenatal oral vitamin K 10 to 20 mg daily from 36 weeks gestation may be considered, although evidence is inadequate for routine use |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | epilepsy#44 | `05ac2372` |  | A representative status epilepticus sequence: provide basic life support with oxygen and maintenance of BP and blood glucose; FIRST give a FAST-ACTING BENZODIAZEPINE -- midazolam, diazepam, clonazepam or lorazepam -- with the choice largely depending on whether IV access is available; if seizures continue or to prevent recurrence give a LONG-ACTING antiseizure drug such as IV phenytoin, valproate, phenobarbital or levetiracetam; if status persists give ANAESTHETIC DOSES of thiopental, midazolam or propofol, with assisted ventilation usually required because of the risk of severe respiratory depression |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | epilepsy#45 | `c41cb172` |  | For ALCOHOL WITHDRAWAL SEIZURES, monitor people at high risk for signs of withdrawal by pulse rate, BP and mental state; BENZODIAZEPINES ARE THE PREFERRED TREATMENT, starting a loading dose regimen at the first signs of withdrawal, and LARGE DOSES MAY BE REQUIRED because of increased hepatic metabolism and CNS tolerance |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | epilepsy#48 | `1cc0fbf7` |  | For phenytoin, carbamazepine and phenobarbital it may be useful to monitor plasma concentrations JUST AFTER A SEIZURE, if dose-dependent adverse effects occur, AFTER A CHANGE IN DOSAGE, or if interacting drugs are started or stopped |

| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anti-infectives-general-principles#14 | `ea9c7d4f` |  | AMBULATORY IV treatment enables patients to complete prolonged IV treatment at home or as an outpatient, with patients usually selected and monitored by INFECTIOUS DISEASES SPECIALISTS; suitable conditions include soft tissue and bone infections, endocarditis, bacteraemia and intra-abdominal infections, and drugs requiring multiple daily doses can be given by CONTINUOUS INFUSION, for example by an elastomeric pump worn by the patient |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anti-infectives-general-principles#16 | `e73af111` |  | USUAL DOSE SCHEDULES ARE ADEQUATE FOR MOST INFECTIONS, AND IN GENERAL SICKER PEOPLE DO NOT REQUIRE HIGHER DOSES THAN PEOPLE WITH LESS SEVERE INFECTION |

| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#2 | `1ccae9dd` | [General principles: anticancer drugs](https://amhonline.amh.net.au/chapters/anticancer-drugs/general-principles-anticancer-drugs) | ANTICANCER DRUGS HAVE A NARROW THERAPEUTIC RANGE AND SHOULD BE PROVIDED ONLY BY APPROPRIATELY TRAINED, ACCREDITED AND AUTHORISED STAFF |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#4 | `ea4c13b9` |  | SCREEN ALL PATIENTS FOR HEPATITIS B, as there is a risk of REACTIVATION with anticancer drugs that cause myelo- or immunosuppression; seek specialist advice if positive as antiviral prophylaxis may be required, and MONITOR FOR CLINICAL AND LABORATORY SIGNS OF HEPATITIS DURING, AND FOR UP TO 12 MONTHS AFTER, TREATMENT |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#7 | `190e25db` |  | ALTHOUGH SOME ANTICANCER DRUGS ARE ASSOCIATED WITH INFERTILITY, IT IS STILL IMPORTANT TO ENSURE EFFECTIVE CONTRACEPTION -- INCLUDING FOR THE PARTNERS OF TREATED MALES -- during, and for a period after stopping, anticancer treatment; the length of time depends on the drug and its teratogenic potential, and contraception may also be continued to delay pregnancy until the risk of recurrence is lower |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#8 | `741c233e` |  | Anticancer drugs are GENERALLY AVOIDED DURING THE FIRST TRIMESTER as they may increase the risk of MAJOR CONGENITAL MALFORMATIONS, ESTIMATED RISK 10 TO 20%, as well as miscarriage and fetal death -- though in some cases such as ACUTE LEUKAEMIA it may not be possible to delay treatment |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#9 | `ba12d3d5` |  | Treatment during the SECOND AND THIRD TRIMESTERS is generally considered acceptable as the risk of malformations does not appear to be increased, HOWEVER the risk of FETAL GROWTH RESTRICTION, LOW BIRTH WEIGHT AND STILLBIRTH may be increased |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#12 | `ae2b8f33` |  | Myelosuppression affects red cells, white cells and platelets. RED CELLS LIVE 120 DAYS so anaemia does not usually occur until after repeated doses; NEUTROPHILS remain in the circulation UP TO 12 HOURS before migrating into tissues where they survive 1 TO 3 DAYS; PLATELETS live 7 TO 10 DAYS -- so WHITE CELLS AND PLATELETS ARE AFFECTED FIRST but generally recover rapidly |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#13 | `7a03dba5` |  | Monitor the complete blood count during myelosuppressive treatment and watch for ANAEMIA (fatigue, dizziness, feeling faint, shortness of breath), THROMBOCYTOPENIA (bleeding or bruising) and the complications of NEUTROPENIA (infection or FEVER ABOVE 38.5 DEGREES CELSIUS); MYELOSUPPRESSION IS THE MOST COMMON REASON TO DELAY CHEMOTHERAPY AND REDUCE THE DOSAGE |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#16 | `25d41205` |  | BROAD-SPECTRUM ANTIBACTERIALS, empirical initially, are indicated in FEBRILE NEUTROPENIA -- refer to local protocols; PROPHYLAXIS FOR PNEUMOCYSTIS JIROVECII PNEUMONIA may be indicated in haematological malignancies, some solid tumours, haemopoietic stem cell transplantation, and with selected chemotherapy regimens or PROLONGED, HIGH DOSES OF CORTICOSTEROIDS |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#17 | `ca08e78b` |  | ACUTE chemotherapy-induced nausea and vomiting occurs in the FIRST 24 HOURS and generally peaks after 5 TO 6 HOURS; DELAYED occurs MORE THAN 24 HOURS after a dose and is common with CISPLATIN, CARBOPLATIN, CYCLOPHOSPHAMIDE and DOXORUBICIN, with delayed vomiting due to cisplatin usually peaking after 48 TO 72 HOURS and lasting 6 TO 7 DAYS |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#19 | `2fbb4458` |  | AMH's antiemetic bands by EMETOGENIC RISK for IV anticancer drugs: HIGH RISK above 90% requires pretreatment with a 5HT3 antagonist, DEXAMETHASONE and a substance P antagonist, with olanzapine optionally added, plus prophylaxis for delayed nausea; MODERATE RISK 30 to 90% requires dexamethasone and a 5HT3 antagonist; LOW RISK 10 to 30% requires dexamethasone, metoclopramide or a 5HT3 antagonist and no delayed prophylaxis; MINIMAL RISK below 10% requires no pretreatment unless there is a history of nausea and vomiting |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#20 | `e407b295` |  | For ORAL anticancer drugs, moderate-to-high emetogenic risk above 30% requires pretreatment with a 5HT3 antagonist, and minimal-to-low risk below 30% requires none unless there is a history of nausea and vomiting |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#23 | `62eb3366` |  | BASIC ORAL CARE -- soft toothbrush, flossing, salt or sodium bicarbonate mouthwashes -- is important for mucosal health ALTHOUGH NOT PROVEN TO PREVENT OR TREAT oral mucositis; chlorhexidine mouthwash may be used for anti-plaque and antifungal properties BUT EVIDENCE FOR PREVENTING ORAL MUCOSITIS IS CONFLICTING, and evidence of effectiveness for topical anaesthetics such as lidocaine 2% oral liquid is LACKING |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#24 | `4d7c592d` |  | ORAL COOLING WITH ICE CHIPS DURING CHEMOTHERAPY ADMINISTRATION PREVENTS OR REDUCES ORAL MUCOSITIS in patients receiving BOLUS DOSES OF FLUOROURACIL OR HIGH-DOSE MELPHALAN; PALIFERMIN, available via the SAS, reduces severity and duration of severe oral mucositis in haematological cancer patients receiving high-dose chemotherapy and total body irradiation before stem cell transplant |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#26 | `708ebb26` |  | HAND-FOOT SYNDROME, palmar-plantar erythrodysaesthesia, is most frequently associated with CAPECITABINE, DOXORUBICIN by continuous infusion or liposomal, CYTARABINE and FLUOROURACIL; it appears DOSE-DEPENDENT, PALMS ARE AFFECTED MORE THAN SOLES, and it presents with dysaesthesia progressing to swelling, pain, erythema and possibly desquamation, USUALLY RESOLVING WITHIN 2 WEEKS IF THE DRUG IS STOPPED. LOSS OF FINGERPRINTS HAS ALSO BEEN REPORTED WITH CAPECITABINE |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#27 | `7c6ceac3` |  | A similar reaction occurs with some KINASE INHIBITORS such as sorafenib and sunitinib, however lesions affect the SOLES MORE THAN PALMS and tend to be more localised and hyperkeratotic; the most effective treatments for hand-foot syndrome are DOSE REDUCTION, INCREASING THE DOSING INTERVAL OR STOPPING THE DRUG |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#28 | `09d21fe4` |  | EGFR INHIBITORS such as cetuximab, erlotinib and gefitinib COMMONLY cause a PAPULOPUSTULAR RASH, usually on the face and upper body, WITHIN THE FIRST 2 TO 4 WEEKS; FOR SOME EGFR INHIBITORS THE OCCURRENCE AND/OR SEVERITY OF THE RASH MAY BE ASSOCIATED WITH IMPROVED RESPONSE OR SURVIVAL |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#29 | `e4c7cb47` |  | Other EGFR inhibitor skin effects include itch, dry skin and fissures, with PARONYCHIA, nail disorders and abnormal hair growth becoming apparent AFTER 1 TO 2 MONTHS; treatment of rash may include topical corticosteroids, topical antibacterials such as clindamycin, or oral antibacterials, and PROPHYLACTIC TETRACYCLINES SUCH AS DOXYCYCLINE HAVE BEEN TRIED BUT EVIDENCE IS CONFLICTING |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#31 | `47cfc954` |  | Tumour lysis syndrome is more common with some haematological malignancies such as acute leukaemias and non-Hodgkin lymphomas, with tumours of high proliferative rate, large burden or high sensitivity to the drug, WITH THE FIRST CYCLE OF CHEMOTHERAPY -- usually WITHIN 12 TO 72 HOURS after administration, although sometimes BEFORE chemotherapy -- and in patients with decreased urinary flow, pre-existing hyperuricaemia, renal impairment, dehydration or acidic urine |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#32 | `b0c26063` |  | PREVENTION OF TUMOUR LYSIS SYNDROME IS IMPORTANT: increased hydration, sometimes with diuretics to maintain urine output, promotes urate and phosphate excretion; ALLOPURINOL STARTED BEFORE CHEMOTHERAPY prevents hyperuricaemia in at-risk patients, and RASBURICASE may be used in those unable to take allopurinol or at high risk; adequate hydration alone may be appropriate if risk is low |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#36 | `c2e22a96` |  | Doses quoted in AMH for anticancer drugs are NOT COMPREHENSIVE and are included ONLY WHERE THE DOSE IS WELL ESTABLISHED; treatment protocols should be consulted as doses in single-agent versus combination therapy are OFTEN QUITE DIFFERENT AND PROTOCOLS MAY CHANGE FREQUENTLY |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#37 | `7eebac7d` |  | Doses are often based on weight or BODY SURFACE AREA, HOWEVER FOR MANY ANTICANCER DRUGS ADJUSTING DOSES ACCORDING TO BSA DOES NOT REDUCE INTER-INDIVIDUAL PHARMACOKINETIC VARIABILITY IN ADULTS |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | anticancer-drugs-general-principles#38 | `4df35938` |  | When calculating body surface area: USE THE SAME CALCULATION METHOD, for example the MOSTELLER EQUATION, WITHIN A HOSPITAL; DO NOT USE PRINTED TABLES OR SLIDE-RULES; DO NOT CAP BSA, for example at 2 square metres; and USE ACTUAL WEIGHT IN OBESE PATIENTS, as alternative estimates of weight MAY RESULT IN UNDERDOSING |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | immunomodulators-general-principles#15 | `d3002731` | [General principles: immunomodulators](https://amhonline.amh.net.au/chapters/immunomodulators-anti-inflammatories/general-principles-immunomodulators) | EXAMINE SKIN REGULARLY FOR SIGNS OF CANCER; patients should avoid direct sun exposure, use a sunscreen with a high SPF of 30 or 50+, and wear a broad-brimmed hat and suitable clothing outdoors |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | immunomodulators-general-principles#18 | `5b8c91f0` |  | The INTENSITY OF IMMUNOSUPPRESSION IS INITIALLY HIGH but tends to be reduced to a maintenance level determined by individual factors and the organ type; the intensity required to preserve transplant function is BALANCED AGAINST THE RISK OF POST-TRANSPLANT SKIN CANCER, LYMPHOMA, INFECTION AND END-ORGAN TOXICITY |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | immunomodulators-general-principles#19 | `e653a38f` |  | In ACUTE CELLULAR rejection, HIGH-DOSE CORTICOSTEROIDS are usually preferred, with ANTITHYMOCYTE GLOBULIN used if corticosteroid-resistant rejection occurs |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | dry-eye-and-topical-eye-drug-principles#59 | `9e819952` | **General principles: topical eye drugs (therapeutic topic)** | AMH advises writing the date on the bottle or tube when first opened and discarding it 28 days later unless told otherwise, as the shelf life for preserved eye products is 28 days after opening |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | dry-eye-and-topical-eye-drug-principles#61 | `a3052aff` |  | After instilling a drop, try not to blink, close the eyes and gently press against the inner corner of the eye over the tear duct for 1 to 2 minutes, which increases the effectiveness of the eye drop and helps reduce the amount of medicine reaching the rest of the body |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | dry-eye-and-topical-eye-drug-principles#62 | `a0064477` |  | The eye pouch will be full after a single drop, and if another eye drop is needed at the same time of day, wait at least 5 minutes before using it |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | dry-eye-and-topical-eye-drug-principles#64 | `816197a9` |  | For eye ointment squeeze a small amount of about 1 cm length along the pouch then blink several times to spread it, and if drops are used at a similar time of day the ointment should be used last |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | dry-eye-and-topical-eye-drug-principles#68 | `352de92d` |  | AMH states that in general there are 20 drops in each mL of an eye drop |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | topical-treatment-of-skin-conditions#25 | `711a730b` | **General principles: topical treatment of skin conditions (therapeutic topic)** | AMH advises that when applying more than one product to the same area of skin, sufficient time should be allowed for absorption between applications |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | topical-treatment-of-skin-conditions#29 | `8b028d12` |  | AMH's suggested weekly quantities table, based on twice daily application for 1 week, gives for patients over 10 years 20 g for face and neck, 30 g for arm and hand, 55 g for leg and foot, 50 g for front of trunk and 50 g for back and buttocks |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | topical-treatment-of-skin-conditions#30 | `1f90f511` |  | AMH's suggested weekly quantities table gives for children aged 3 to 12 months 7 g for face and neck, 7 g for arm and hand, 10 g for leg and foot, 7 g for front of trunk and 10 g for back and buttocks, with quantities increasing through the 1 to 3, 3 to 6 and 6 to 10 year age bands |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | topical-treatment-of-skin-conditions#31 | `a7efea31` |  | AMH states that for corticosteroids, calcipotriol and pimecrolimus the suggested weekly quantities should not be exceeded without specialist recommendation |

| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | angina#55 | `7b416f8d` |  | For prevention of chronic angina, apply one glyceryl trinitrate 5 mg/24 hours patch once daily for approximately 12 to 14 hours each day, increasing if necessary to a maximum of two 10 mg/24 hours patches once daily |

| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#1 | `5c958029` | [Hypertension](https://amhonline.amh.net.au/chapters/cardiovascular-drugs/antihypertensives/hypertension) | Observational studies show that the risk of cardiovascular morbidity and mortality rises as BP increases FROM 115/75 mmHg; age, male sex and other cardiovascular risk factors contribute |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#2 | `9e6cf575` |  | THE DISTINCTION BETWEEN NORMOTENSION AND HYPERTENSION IS ARBITRARY: normotension is generally considered a clinic BP of 120/80 mmHg and below, and hypertension as above 140/90 mmHg |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#7 | `553c0995` |  | Automated office BP measurement, which may be taken unobserved by staff, gives a SUBSTANTIALLY LOWER systolic BP -- by about 10 to 20 mmHg -- than conventional clinic readings; this method was used in the SPRINT study and is CURRENTLY NOT OFTEN PRACTISED IN AUSTRALIA |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#10 | `820102c5` |  | Lifestyle change can reduce BP and potentially avoid or delay the need for antihypertensives: REGULAR AEROBIC EXERCISE CAN REDUCE DAYTIME SYSTOLIC BP BY UP TO 3.2 mmHg, and A WEIGHT LOSS OF 10 kg IN AN OVERWEIGHT PERSON MAY REDUCE SYSTOLIC BP BY 6 TO 10 mmHg |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#12 | `73f83d51` |  | Assess cardiovascular risk in all adults without known cardiovascular disease aged 45 to 79 years, FROM 35 YEARS in people with diabetes and FROM 30 YEARS in Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander people; a guideline and 5-year risk calculator are at cvdcheck.org.au |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#13 | `68ac3996` |  | IN ADULTS WITHOUT KNOWN CARDIOVASCULAR DISEASE, CONSIDER DRUG TREATMENT ACCORDING TO ESTIMATED RISK RATHER THAN TARGETING BP ALONE -- although treat those with systolic BP above 160 mmHg or diastolic above 100 mmHg regardless |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#14 | `be1bf53f` |  | By estimated 5-year risk: HIGH RISK, 10% or more, or clinically determined high risk -- start antihypertensive AND lipid-lowering therapy unless clinically inappropriate; INTERMEDIATE RISK, 5% to under 10% -- consider drug treatment; LOW RISK, under 5% -- drug treatment is not routinely recommended |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#15 | `2fd096df` |  | Australian guidelines recommend treating uncomplicated hypertension to a target of BELOW 140/90 mmHg, or lower if tolerated |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#17 | `03c9eb48` |  | A lower target of systolic BP below 120 mmHg can be considered in selected cardiovascular populations at high risk, provided there is close monitoring for adverse effects such as hypotension and electrolyte abnormalities |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#18 | `cc709143` |  | In the SPRINT study, patients at high cardiovascular risk had fewer cardiovascular events and reduced mortality -- BUT MORE TREATMENT-RELATED ADVERSE EVENTS -- with a target systolic BP below 120 mmHg compared with below 140 mmHg; its generalisability may be LIMITED by its use of automated office BP and its exclusion criteria, which excluded age under 50, diabetes, heart failure and history of stroke |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#19 | `94945815` |  | Generally START WITH A SINGLE DRUG AT THE LOWEST RECOMMENDED DOSE; this will satisfactorily reduce BP in about 25 to 50% of people |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#20 | `8582d561` |  | For uncomplicated hypertension, unless there is a contraindication or a specific indication for another drug, first consider an ACE INHIBITOR (or sartan), OR a DIHYDROPYRIDINE CALCIUM CHANNEL BLOCKER, OR -- IF 65 OR OLDER -- A LOW-DOSE THIAZIDE DIURETIC |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#22 | `ef2a5a5a` |  | Generally allow 4 TO 6 WEEKS to assess the response to treatment; consider a shorter review period for marked hypertension or high cardiovascular risk |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#28 | `582400a3` |  | Thiazide diuretics are generally well tolerated and are FIRST-LINE FOR HYPERTENSION IN THOSE OVER 65; because of their association with NEW-ONSET DIABETES they are NO LONGER RECOMMENDED AS FIRST-LINE MONOTHERAPY IN YOUNGER PATIENTS |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#38 | `80316d63` |  | If the target is still not reached on two well-tolerated drugs, increase the dose of one NON-THIAZIDE agent towards the maximum recommended, and if that is still inadequate increase the dose of the other |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#39 | `707751d8` |  | If BP remains above target despite maximum doses of at least 2 appropriate agents, consider POOR COMPLIANCE, HIGH SALT INTAKE, SECONDARY HYPERTENSION including drug-induced, VOLUME OVERLOAD and SLEEP APNOEA, which can cause treatment resistance; three or more antihypertensives from different classes may be needed |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#40 | `a2a8058a` |  | RESISTANT HYPERTENSION -- uncontrolled BP despite 3 or more optimally tolerated antihypertensives INCLUDING A DIURETIC -- occurs in MORE THAN 10% OF PATIENTS; consider PRIMARY ALDOSTERONISM especially if hypokalaemia is present, and seek specialist advice after ruling out compliance and other secondary causes |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#47 | `4a03a2fd` |  | ELDERLY: start treatment at the lowest dose and titrate slowly |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#48 | `ee8093c1` |  | In a SPRINT subgroup of selected ambulatory patients aged over 75 -- excluding diabetes, heart failure and history of stroke -- lowering systolic BP to below 120 mmHg significantly reduced the risk of cardiovascular and all-cause mortality; NOTE THE STUDY USED AUTOMATED OFFICE BP, where a target below 120 mmHg is comparable to a CLINIC systolic BP of 130 to 140 mmHg |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#50 | `e6baaa6c` |  | CORONARY ARTERY DISEASE: monitor BP closely on antihypertensives -- in an international cohort study of hypertensive patients with stable coronary artery disease BOTH high BP (systolic above 140 or diastolic above 80 mmHg) AND low BP (systolic below 120 or diastolic below 70 mmHg) were associated with increased cardiovascular morbidity and mortality |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#51 | `4f5a8f29` |  | DIABETES: a BP target below 140/90 mmHg is recommended; if preventing stroke is a priority, consider lowering to a systolic below 120 mmHg provided the patient is closely monitored for hypotension and acute kidney injury |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#53 | `dd98fc5b` |  | HYPERTENSIVE CRISIS: AVOID PRECIPITOUS BP REDUCTION -- more than 25% over 2 hours -- to prevent serious events such as MI and stroke; monitor BP continuously and seek specialist advice, the aim being to limit acute end-organ damage |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#55 | `6e6dda9f` |  | To reduce BP over 2 to 6 hours consider IV labetalol, sodium nitroprusside, glyceryl trinitrate, hydralazine or clevidipine; beta-blockers may be needed to control reflex tachycardia; IN MOST CASES AIM TOWARDS 160/100 mmHg RATHER THAN NORMAL BP, THEN WAIT 24 HOURS BEFORE LOWERING FURTHER |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#56 | `3977c435` |  | For less urgent BP reduction use oral antihypertensives and follow up within 24 to 72 hours |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#57 | `5d650edc` |  | HYPERTENSION IN PREGNANCY: for stable chronic, gestational or non-severe pre-eclampsia a target BP below 135/85 mmHg is recommended; first-line oral drugs are METHYLDOPA, LABETALOL and NIFEDIPINE, and oral hydralazine can also be considered |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | hypertension#59 | `8f75fcbf` |  | For SEVERE hypertension in pregnancy -- systolic above 160 or diastolic above 110 mmHg -- oral nifedipine, as a conventional tablet available through the SAS, and parenteral labetalol and hydralazine are used for urgent BP reduction, following local protocols |

Thiazides oral daily: chlortalidone 12.5-25 mg, hydrochlorothiazide 12.5-50 mg, indapamide 1.25-2.5 mg or MR 1.5 mg (first-line over 65). |

|  | hypertension#67 | `1f1c1124` |  | Other second-line oral options: atenolol 25-100 mg daily; prazosin 0.5 mg twice daily increasing to 1-10 mg twice daily; methyldopa 125 mg twice daily up to 500 mg three times daily; moxonidine 200 micrograms daily up to 300 micrograms twice daily (avoid in heart failure); clonidine 50 micrograms twice daily up to 300 micrograms twice daily; minoxidil 5 mg daily up to 30 mg twice daily with a loop diuretic and a beta blocker. |
|  | hypertension#68 | `1d851180` |  | Stopping a beta blocker or clonidine: halve the dose every 2-3 days until atenolol 25 mg daily / metoprolol 25 mg twice daily / clonidine 50-75 micrograms daily, then stop. |

| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | immunisation#11 | `89833859` |  | PREGNANCY: ALL PREGNANT WOMEN ARE RECOMMENDED TO RECEIVE INACTIVATED INFLUENZA VACCINE AT ANY STAGE OF EACH PREGNANCY, PARTICULARLY IF THE SECOND OR THIRD TRIMESTER WILL BE IN THE INFLUENZA SEASON; PERTUSSIS AS dTpa EACH PREGNANCY, PREFERABLY BETWEEN 20 AND 32 WEEKS GESTATION; AND RSV VACCINE EACH PREGNANCY BETWEEN 28 AND 36 WEEKS GESTATION. ⚠️ IMMUNISATION DURING PREGNANCY PROTECTS THE NEWBORN UNTIL THEY CAN RECEIVE THEIR FIRST VACCINATION -- AT 6 WEEKS FOR PERTUSSIS, AT 6 MONTHS FOR INFLUENZA |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | immunisation#12 | `12632f36` |  | ⚠️ LIVE VACCINATIONS SUCH AS MMR SHOULD BE AVOIDED DURING PREGNANCY due to theoretical risk of disease transmission, and WOMEN ARE ADVISED TO AVOID PREGNANCY FOR 28 DAYS AFTER VACCINATION. ⚠️ HOWEVER, INADVERTENT EXPOSURE JUST BEFORE OR DURING PREGNANCY HAS NOT BEEN SHOWN TO CAUSE FETAL HARM AND IS NOT A REASON TO TERMINATE THE PREGNANCY -- SEEK SPECIALIST ADVICE |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | immunisation#14 | `9cf438b0` |  | CATCH-UP VACCINATION: ⚠️ OFFER CHILDREN WITH A POOR HISTORY OF VACCINATION THE FULL COURSE APPROPRIATE FOR THEIR AGE -- SERIOUS ADVERSE EFFECTS ARE UNLIKELY IF EXTRA VACCINE DOSES ARE GIVEN. ⚠️ THE IMMUNE RESPONSE IS UNAFFECTED BY EXCEEDING THE RECOMMENDED INTERVALS BETWEEN VACCINATIONS -- THERE IS NO NEED TO GIVE EXTRA DOSES OR RESTART THE SCHEDULE. WRITTEN EVIDENCE OF VACCINATIONS IS IMPORTANT AS RECALL CAN BE FAULTY. A CATCH-UP CALCULATOR IS AVAILABLE for children under 10 years on the National Immunisation Program |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | immunisation#17 | `e7ac44e8` |  | ⚠️ THE CORTICOSTEROID IMMUNOSUPPRESSION THRESHOLD, WHICH IS THE ONE NUMBER A GP IS MOST LIKELY TO NEED: live vaccines are contraindicated in those taking SYSTEMIC CORTICOSTEROIDS EQUIVALENT TO PREDNISOLONE ABOVE 2 mg/kg DAILY FOR MORE THAN 14 DAYS, OR ABOVE 1 mg/kg DAILY FOR MORE THAN 1 MONTH IN CHILDREN, OR PREDNISOLONE ABOVE 20 mg DAILY IN ADULTS. ⚠️ SEEK SPECIALIST ADVICE IF TAKING CHRONIC LOW-DOSE CORTICOSTEROIDS SUCH AS 5 mg PREDNISOLONE DAILY, AS THE DEGREE OF IMMUNOSUPPRESSION CAN VARY. ⚠️ PEOPLE USING INHALED CORTICOSTEROIDS CAN BE SAFELY VACCINATED |

| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | inhalers-and-nebulisers#9 | `822bbe5e` |  | SPACERS ARE RECOMMENDED FOR: children, using a SMALL-VOLUME spacer for children under 5 years with a face mask if necessary; adults with poor hand-breath coordination; ACUTE ASTHMA ATTACKS; patients using inhaled corticosteroids, particularly at higher doses; and to reduce oropharyngeal adverse effects -- candidiasis, dysphonia, frequent cough reflex |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | inhalers-and-nebulisers#10 | `18dadad5` |  | LARGE-VOLUME SPACERS of 650 to 850 mL are MORE EFFICIENT than small-volume spacers of 60 to 160 mL but less convenient, and are UNSUITABLE FOR CHILDREN UNDER 5 YEARS because of their insufficient tidal volume |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | inhalers-and-nebulisers#11 | `4d9a68cd` |  | Wash spacers BEFORE FIRST USE, then ONCE A MONTH: wash in warm water and dish-washing detergent, DO NOT RINSE, allow to AIR DRY -- do not dry with a cloth as this can make medication stick to the spacer and make it less effective -- and check for cracks and that the valve is functioning |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | angina#63 | `e449979e` | [Isosorbide dinitrate](https://amhonline.amh.net.au/chapters/cardiovascular-drugs/drugs-angina/nitrates/isosorbide-dinitrate) | Isosorbide dinitrate for treatment of acute angina, sublingual 5 to 10 mg; for prevention before a precipitating activity, sublingual 5 to 10 mg taken 10 minutes before the activity |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | angina#64 | `8fa4f9d9` |  | The only PBS-listed isosorbide dinitrate product is a 5 mg sublingual tablet, which may be taken sublingually, the most common route, or orally |

|  | urinary-alkalinisation-and-acidification#7 | `28be25f7` | [Kidney and ureteric stones in adults](https://app.tg.org.au/viewTopic?etgAccess=true&guidelinePage=Kidney%20and%20Urinary&topicfile=kidney-overview&guidelinename=auto&sectionId=c_KUG_Kidney-and-ureteric-stones-in-adults_topic_13) | For uric acid stone prevention, target urinary pH 6 to 7 via diet plus citrate salts (potassium citrate preferred) or sodium bicarbonate if citrate unsuitable; citrate salts also for hypocitraturia in calcium stone formers and for cystine stones. |
|  | kidney-stones#10 | `7d553174` | [Kidney and ureteric stones in adults](https://app.tg.org.au/viewTopic?etgAccess=true&guidelinePage=Kidney%20and%20Urinary&topicfile=kidney-overview&guidelinename=auto&sectionId=c_KUG_Kidney-and-ureteric-stones-in-adults_topic_2) | Recurrent calcium stones with persistent hypercalciuria (specialist advice): chlortalidone 12.5 mg orally daily up to 50 mg, or hydrochlorothiazide 25 mg daily up to 50 mg twice daily, or indapamide 2.5 mg daily (all off-label); avoid in gout. |
|  | kidney-stones#11 | `98fe624e` |  | Hypocitraturia or uric acid/cystine stones: potassium citrate usually 4 to 8 g daily, titrated to urine pH and citrate (target pH 6-7 for uric acid, at least 7.5 for cystine); cystinuria also needs at least 3 L fluid daily. |
|  | kidney-stones#12 | `3729d23e` |  | Urgent ED transfer for fever, UTI, severe pain, pregnancy or single kidney; refer to urology if stone over 6 mm, eGFR 60 or less, colic over 2 weeks, or not passed by 4 weeks; UTI with obstruction needs urgent decompression. |
|  | kidney-stones#13 | `feb61d18` |  | Offer metabolic evaluation (24-hour urine, stone analysis, bloods) for recurrent, bilateral, non-calcium or single-kidney stones, family history, age under 18 or high-risk occupations. |

| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | kidney-stones#4 | `820d33e3` |  | SELECTIVE ALPHA-BLOCKERS are sometimes used in SELECTED PATIENTS WITH LOWER URETERAL STONES UNDER 10 mm to help stone passage -- ⚠️ ONLY PATIENTS WITH STONES 5-10 mm IN SIZE ARE LIKELY TO BENEFIT, DUE TO THE HIGH SPONTANEOUS PASSAGE RATE OF UP TO 89% FOR SMALLER STONES. SURGERY MAY BE REQUIRED |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | kidney-stones#5 | `6e3843bf` |  | PREVENTION: ⚠️ THE RECURRENCE RATE IS HIGH. INCREASING FLUID INTAKE -- TO ACHIEVE A URINE OUTPUT ABOVE 2 L DAILY IF POSSIBLE -- IS IMPORTANT IN PREVENTION. Dietary measures such as RESTRICTING SALT INTAKE may also help, and ⚠️ NORMAL DIETARY CALCIUM INTAKE IS RECOMMENDED -- NOT RESTRICTED |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | kidney-stones#8 | `5eb8bc76` |  | ⚠️ DRUGS THAT AFFECT STONE RISK: WHETHER ASCORBIC ACID INCREASES THE RISK OF CALCIUM OXALATE STONE FORMATION IS UNCLEAR, BUT DOSES ABOVE 1 g DAILY SHOULD BE AVOIDED. The effect of CALCIUM AND/OR VITAMIN D SUPPLEMENTATION on recurrence IS UNCLEAR -- use with caution, and ADVISE THAT CALCIUM SUPPLEMENTS ARE TAKEN WITH FOOD TO BIND OXALATE AND DECREASE URINARY OXALATE EXCRETION |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | kidney-stones#9 | `d6aec03a` |  | ⚠️ CARBONIC ANHYDRASE INHIBITORS -- eg ACETAZOLAMIDE, TOPIRAMATE -- CAN INCREASE THE RISK OF CALCIUM PHOSPHATE STONES. PROBENECID CAN INCREASE THE RISK OF URIC ACID STONES. ⚠️ RENALLY EXCRETED DRUGS WITH LOW SOLUBILITY AT URINE pH, eg CIPROFLOXACIN, MAY CRYSTALLISE IN THE URINARY TRACT FORMING STONES, ESPECIALLY AT HIGH DOSES |

| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | bipolar-disorder#30 | `a5a73f8f` |  | Avoid lithium or use it carefully where renal clearance may be decreased, FOR EXAMPLE WITH NSAIDs; toxicity relates to serum concentration and the risk is UNACCEPTABLY HIGH in severe renal impairment and when renal function is unstable; reduce the dose even in relatively mild renal dysfunction |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | bipolar-disorder#35 | `af1dec8a` |  | LONG-TERM LITHIUM TREATMENT OF MORE THAN 10 YEARS INCREASES THE RISK OF RENAL TUMOURS, including cancers and oncocytomas |

| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | bipolar-disorder#38 | `cba8095b` |  | DO NOT STOP LITHIUM ABRUPTLY: withdraw gradually OVER AT LEAST 4 WEEKS to avoid relapse |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | bipolar-disorder#39 | `82c9ebed` |  | Lithium's ONSET OF ACTION MAY BE DELAYED FOR 6 TO 10 DAYS, so in severe mania consider adding a benzodiazepine or antipsychotic, using oral agents where possible with careful compliance supervision, and withdrawing them after acute mania has resolved; MOST PATIENTS REQUIRE HOSPITALISATION FOR ACUTE MANIA |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | bipolar-disorder#44 | `3f9712aa` |  | LITHIUM CLEARANCE INCREASES IN THE SECOND HALF OF PREGNANCY so dose adjustments may be needed; use the lowest dose possible, and AFTER THE BIRTH GIVE THE PRECONCEPTION DOSE as clearance returns to normal immediately |

| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | bipolar-disorder#47 | `ce88b0fd` |  | The lithium products listed are 250 mg scored tablets (Lithicarb) and 450 mg controlled-release tablets (Quilonum SR) |

|  | mouth-and-throat-conditions#44 | `60699526` | [Oral and oropharyngeal infection caused by Candida and related species](https://app.tg.org.au/viewTopic?etgAccess=true&guidelinePage=Antibiotic&topicfile=bartonella-infections&guidelinename=auto&sectionId=c_ABG_Oral-and-oropharyngeal-infection-caused-by-Candida-and-related-species_topic_2) | Oral candidiasis in neonates: nystatin 100 000 units/mL 1 mL 4 times daily after feeds for about 7 days (continue 2 days after resolution), or miconazole 2% gel 1 mL 2-4 times daily for 7-14 days; children: miconazole gel 1.25 mL or nystatin 1 mL 4 times daily. |

| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | diarrhoea#37 | `cb0e3d04` |  | The only PBS-listed oral rehydration product AMH lists is a 4.9 g sachet of glucose with sodium acid citrate, sodium chloride and potassium chloride, restricted to Aboriginal or Torres Strait Islander patients or to rehydration in intestinal failure; SIMILAR PRODUCTS ARE AVAILABLE OVER THE COUNTER |
|  | mouth-and-throat-conditions#45 | `6c1f4b68` | [Oral ulcers](https://app.tg.org.au/viewTopic?etgAccess=true&guidelinePage=Oral%20and%20Dental&topicfile=dental-caries&guidelinename=auto&sectionId=c_DTG_Oral-ulcers_topic_2) | Oral ulcer pain: benzydamine 1% gel to the ulcer 2- to 3-hourly as needed (age 6 or older); lidocaine 2% viscous up to 15 mL rinse 3-hourly (max 8 doses/day) in adults; avoid salicylate gels. |
|  | orthostatic-hypotension-and-pots#38 | `9853fe1d` | [Orthostatic hypotension](https://app.tg.org.au/viewTopic?etgAccess=true&guidelinePage=Cardiovascular&topicfile=cardiovascular-disease-risk-stratification&guidelinename=auto&sectionId=c_CVG_Orthostatic-hypotensiontopic_2) | Nondrug measures are the mainstay: avoid heat and prolonged standing, rise slowly, reduce/stagger exacerbating drugs, leg-tensing and crossing, avoid alcohol early in the day, front-load fluids, drink 300-500 mL water before breakfast, abdominal binder (knee/thigh stockings ineffective), minimise daytime bed rest, exercise program. |
|  | orthostatic-hypotension-and-pots#39 | `0d8ffdd5` |  | For hypertension coexisting with OH prefer an ACEI, ARB or dihydropyridine CCB over alpha blockers, beta blockers or diuretics; consider afternoon/evening dosing if nocturnal supine hypertension; do not stop appropriate antihypertensives without trying nondrug measures first. |
|  | orthostatic-hypotension-and-pots#40 | `05dafb3a` |  | PoTS and vasovagal syncope in young adults: leg-tensing manoeuvres, morning hydration and exercise; refer to a specialist if drug therapy is needed. |
|  | otitis-externa-and-ear-wax#41 | `5b3a97fa` | [Otitis externa](https://app.tg.org.au/viewTopic?etgAccess=true&guidelinePage=Antibiotic&topicfile=otitis-externa&guidelinename=Antibiotic) | Acute localised otitis externa (boil): dicloxacillin or flucloxacillin 500 mg (child 12.5 mg/kg) orally 6-hourly for 5 days; erysipelas of pinna: phenoxymethylpenicillin 500 mg 6-hourly 5 days; cefalexin 500 mg 6-hourly (child option); MRSA risk/severe penicillin allergy: trimethoprim+sulfamethoxazole 160+800 mg 12-hourly or clindamycin 450 mg 8-hourly for 5 days. |
|  | otitis-externa-and-ear-wax#42 | `bdf4adf9` |  | Necrotising otitis externa (diabetes, elderly, immunocompromised; pain despite improved canal): urgent ED referral; piperacillin+tazobactam 4+0.5 g IV 6-hourly (continuous infusion if septic shock); cefepime 2 g IV 8-hourly if nonsevere penicillin allergy; prolonged therapy with ID/ENT input. |
|  | otitis-externa-and-ear-wax#43 | `8d269c7d` |  | Analgesia (paracetamol/NSAID) for acute diffuse otitis externa; no oral antibiotics; culture not routinely needed. |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | otitis-externa-and-ear-wax#20 | `9acefb27` | **Otitis externa (therapeutic topic)** | AMH advises keeping the external ear canal dry by meticulous gentle cleansing and drying with tissue spears made of rolled tissue paper, 2 to 4 times daily if possible but at least once daily |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | otitis-externa-and-ear-wax#26 | `f7f8f816` |  | AMH's topical agents table gives dexamethasone with framycetin and gramicidin 3 or 4 times daily and framycetin alone 3 times daily for an intact eardrum, ciprofloxacin with or without hydrocortisone twice daily for a perforated eardrum, flumetasone with clioquinol twice daily and triamcinolone with neomycin, nystatin and gramicidin 2 or 3 times daily where fungal infection is likely, and notes a risk of fungal infection with prolonged use beyond 7 days |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | otitis-externa-and-ear-wax#31 | `c5e14938` |  | AMH advises keeping ears dry during treatment and for 2 weeks after using cotton wool balls smeared with white soft paraffin while showering or bathing and avoiding swimming, then using ear plugs while swimming, showering or bathing, and considering an antiseptic ear drop or drying agent after water exposure |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | otitis-externa-and-ear-wax#33 | `bcf5730d` |  | For administering ear medications AMH advises the patient should receive help if possible, the ear should be clean and dry using rolled tissue paper spears, the patient should lie with the affected ear uppermost, the container may be warmed in a cup of warm water to reduce viscosity, and drops should be instilled directly into the ear canal with the patient remaining in position for 3 to 5 minutes, with gentle massage or pressure on the tragus to aid penetration |
|  | urinary-incontinence#41 | `6d163a73` | [Overactive bladder in adults](https://app.tg.org.au/viewTopic?etgAccess=true&guidelinePage=Kidney%20and%20Urinary&topicfile=kidney-overview&guidelinename=auto&sectionId=c_KUG_Overactive-bladder-in-adults_topic_2) | Mirabegron modified-release 25 to 50 mg orally daily as preferred first drug for OAB (before anticholinergics); max 25 mg in moderate hepatic impairment or eGFR 15 to 29; avoid in severe uncontrolled hypertension; check BP at baseline and within the first week. |
|  | urinary-incontinence#42 | `7ba80863` |  | Anticholinergic options (under 65, with caution): darifenacin MR 7.5 mg daily (up to 15 mg), oxybutynin 2.5 mg 2 or 3 times daily (max 20 mg/day), oxybutynin 3.9 mg/24 h patch twice weekly, solifenacin 5 mg daily (up to 10 mg), tolterodine 1 to 2 mg twice daily. |
|  | urinary-incontinence#43 | `f89d88f0` |  | Specialist combination for partial response: mirabegron 50 mg daily plus solifenacin 5 mg daily; do not combine anticholinergics. |
|  | urinary-incontinence#44 | `b8315dad` |  | Bladder rehabilitation (bladder training to 3 to 4 hourly voids, pelvic floor muscle training for at least 3 months, fluid/caffeine modification) is first line and continued with drug therapy. |
|  | urinary-incontinence#45 | `c04571ef` |  | Peri/postmenopausal females with OAB symptoms and vaginal atrophy: consider intravaginal estrogen. |
|  | urinary-incontinence#46 | `1e2fc036` |  | Refractory OAB: intravesical botulinum toxin A, sacral neuromodulation or percutaneous tibial nerve stimulation via specialists. |
|  | parkinsons-disease#40 | `1cac0169` | [Parkinson disease](https://app.tg.org.au/viewTopic?etgAccess=true&guidelinePage=Neurology&topicfile=parkinson-disease&guidelinename=Neurology) | Other add-on options for motor fluctuations (neurologist-directed): opicapone 50 mg orally at bedtime; safinamide 50 mg daily, 100 mg after 2 weeks if needed; selegiline 2.5 mg daily up to 5 mg twice daily (morning and midday); pramipexole MR 0.375 mg daily. |
|  | parkinsons-disease#41 | `2a03dce3` |  | Wearing off: more frequent evenly spaced levodopa doses or MR levodopa; take levodopa about 1 hour before meals, as high-protein meals impair absorption. |
|  | parkinsons-disease#42 | `cc887f46` |  | Orthostatic hypotension in Parkinson disease: nondrug measures, then fludrocortisone 0.1 mg orally daily, up to 0.2 mg daily. |
|  | parkinsons-disease#43 | `b6d6b9e1` |  | Parkinson psychosis: withdraw anticholinergics, amantadine and dopamine agonists first; if needed low-dose clozapine or quetiapine, or a cholinesterase inhibitor; avoid olanzapine, risperidone, aripiprazole and first-generation antipsychotics. |
|  | parkinsons-disease#44 | `4df7e275` |  | Sialorrhoea awaiting botulinum toxin: amitriptyline 5 mg at night, increasing weekly to max 25 mg. |
|  | parkinsons-disease#45 | `1e0d4b02` |  | Nil-by-mouth: well-controlled patients can miss 2-3 oral doses; otherwise switch to a nonoral route (eg rotigotine patch applied the night before surgery) using a levodopa-equivalent calculator and neurologist advice; never stop abruptly. |
|  | arrhythmias#120 | `8abb497d` | [Paroxysmal supraventricular tachycardia](https://app.tg.org.au/viewTopic?etgAccess=true&guidelinePage=Cardiovascular&topicfile=cardiovascular-disease-risk-stratification&guidelinename=auto&sectionId=c_CVG_Paroxysmal-supraventricular-tachycardiatopic_2) | Acute PSVT after vagal manoeuvres and adenosine: verapamil 5 to 10 mg IV over 2 minutes, repeat once after 30 minutes if tolerated; or metoprolol tartrate 2.5 to 5 mg IV over 2 minutes, repeat after 10 minutes to max 3 doses. |

Severe PID with severe penicillin allergy: gentamicin or tobramycin IV plus azithromycin 500 mg IV daily plus clindamycin 600 mg IV 8-hourly. |

| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | peptic-ulcer-disease#53 | `08b13547` |  | For NSAID-induced ulcers, stop the NSAID excluding low-dose aspirin if possible and substitute paracetamol and non-drug treatment, and treat with a PPI for at least 8 weeks, testing for H. pylori and treating if present, possibly delaying treatment until the ulcer has healed |

| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | angina#96 | `d8b504e0` |  | Perhexiline dose adjustments during chronic treatment should increase by no more than 50 mg daily, because its metabolism is saturable within the clinical dose range and dose changes can produce disproportionate changes in plasma concentration |

| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | perianal-disorders#35 | `efd36ade` | **Perianal disorders (therapeutic topic)** | Simple emollients such as sorbolene cream or zinc oxide ointment applied after cleansing may help pruritus ani, and a short course of 5 to 7 days of hydrocortisone ointment may relieve any inflammation |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | perianal-disorders#44 | `b7883d1a` |  | Use of topical anaesthetics and corticosteroids in perianal disorders should be limited to under 7 days, as local anaesthetics may sensitise perianal skin and topical corticosteroids may exacerbate local infection and cause skin atrophy |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | erectile-dysfunction#30 | `c6a2f0c6` | [Phosphodiesterase 5 inhibitors (genitourinary)](https://amhonline.amh.net.au/chapters/genitourinary-drugs/drugs-sexual-dysfunction/phosphodiesterase-5-inhibitors-genitourinary) | AMH states that if a nitrate is required after a PDE5 inhibitor has been taken, wait at least 24 hours with sildenafil, 12 hours with avanafil and 48 hours with tadalafil before giving the nitrate, that a longer period may be required if elimination is delayed, and to monitor response carefully |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | erectile-dysfunction#31 | `f242032b` |  | AMH advises that treatment with drugs that can cause hypotension, particularly selective alpha-blockers, may add to hypotension caused by PDE5 inhibitors, and that if using with an alpha-blocker the alpha-blocker therapy should be stabilised before starting the PDE5 inhibitor at the lowest starting dose |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | erectile-dysfunction#38 | `aaec14b4` |  | AMH states that in erectile dysfunction PDE5 inhibitor onset is about 30 minutes to 1 hour with avanafil possibly effective within 15 minutes, that avanafil and sildenafil are short-acting being effective for about 6 to 8 hours, and that tadalafil is long-acting for up to 36 hours |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | erectile-dysfunction#45 | `e0a69b51` |  | AMH advises that if response to a PDE5 inhibitor is inadequate, consider whether the timing of the dose before intercourse is optimal given the drug's onset and duration of action, increasing to the maximum dose while keeping in mind this can cause more adverse effects with limited additional efficacy with patients who have diabetes or are post-prostatectomy most likely to benefit, and trying an alternative PDE5 inhibitor |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | warts#27 | `bd02786a` | [Podophyllotoxin](https://amhonline.amh.net.au/chapters/dermatological-drugs/drugs-warts/podophyllotoxin) | AMH gives the podophyllotoxin regimen for adults and children over 2 years as a 7-day treatment cycle applying the liquid twice a day for 3 days followed by no treatment for 4 days, with the cycle repeated as necessary for up to 4 or 5 cycles |

VERY SMALL CHANGES IN THE INTRACELLULAR/EXTRACELLULAR DISTRIBUTION CAN LEAD TO MAJOR CHANGES IN SERUM/PLASMA LEVELS, which may alter the resting membrane potential of excitable tissues and cause ARRHYTHMIAS AND MUSCLE DYSFUNCTION |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | potassium-disturbances#4 | `209f9942` |  | ⚠️ SEVERE HYPERKALAEMIA -- SERUM POTASSIUM ABOVE 6.0 mmol/L -- IS A MEDICAL EMERGENCY THAT CAN CAUSE MUSCLE WEAKNESS, ARRHYTHMIAS AND/OR CARDIAC ARREST. It is MOST COMMONLY CAUSED BY RENAL FAILURE AND IS RARE WHEN RENAL FUNCTION IS NORMAL |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | potassium-disturbances#7 | `88c1e5b6` |  | STABILISE THE CARDIAC CELL MEMBRANE: CALCIUM GLUCONATE OR CALCIUM CHLORIDE BY IV INJECTION counters the cardiac effects of raised potassium. ⚠️ CHECK LOCAL PROTOCOLS TO GUIDE DOSAGE AND ADMINISTRATION -- AMH GIVES NO DOSE. EFFECTS SHOULD BEGIN WITHIN A FEW MINUTES AND LAST 30-60 MINUTES; REPEAT IF NECESSARY. Calcium gluconate is LESS IRRITANT and contains LESS CALCIUM PER GRAM than calcium chloride |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | potassium-disturbances#9 | `e6c1a260` |  | PROMOTE CELLULAR UPTAKE: IV GLUCOSE AND INSULIN promote cellular uptake of potassium -- THE EFFECTS OF INSULIN SHOULD BEGIN WITHIN 10-20 MINUTES AND LAST 4-6 HOURS, and GLUCOSE IS NOT REQUIRED IF HYPERGLYCAEMIC. SALBUTAMOL, NEBULISED AND RARELY IV, causes potassium to shift into cells and may be added -- USUALLY ONLY SHORT TERM IN PATIENTS WITH ONGOING SYMPTOMS OR SERIOUS ECG CHANGES AFTER CALCIUM AND INSULIN/GLUCOSE THERAPY |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | potassium-disturbances#12 | `ea4c3daa` |  | POLYSTYRENE SULFONATE RESINS will SLIGHTLY increase potassium excretion ⚠️ ALTHOUGH THEIR EFFECTIVENESS IS DEBATABLE -- any effect TAKES SOME HOURS, repeated dosing may be required, and IT IS NOT A SUBSTITUTE FOR EMERGENCY MEASURES OR DIALYSIS. PATIROMER is a cation exchange polymer whose ONSET MAY TAKE UP TO 7 HOURS after oral administration; ⚠️ IT IS NOT A SUBSTITUTE FOR EMERGENCY MEASURES AND LONG-TERM SAFETY AND EFFICACY DATA ARE LACKING |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | potassium-disturbances#13 | `cd7fd803` |  | MILD HYPOKALAEMIA -- SERUM POTASSIUM 3.1-3.5 mmol/L -- IS USUALLY ASYMPTOMATIC. ⚠️ SEVERE HYPOKALAEMIA -- BELOW 2.5 mmol/L -- CAN CAUSE MUSCLE WEAKNESS, PALPITATIONS, ARRHYTHMIAS AND/OR SUDDEN CARDIAC DEATH |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | potassium-disturbances#16 | `ceeadb98` |  | SEVERE ACUTE POTASSIUM LOSS may require IV treatment, especially if symptomatic. ⚠️ GIVE IV SLOWLY AS A THOROUGHLY MIXED, DILUTE SOLUTION TO AVOID LIFE-THREATENING ARRHYTHMIAS. MONITOR SERUM POTASSIUM FREQUENTLY -- and fluid balance if giving large volumes -- AND MONITOR ECG |

| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | preterm-labour#2 | `d6e1c4a6` |  | ⚠️ TOCOLYSIS IS GENERALLY NOT INDICATED BEFORE THE FETUS IS VIABLE, WHEN GESTATION IS OVER 34 WEEKS, OR WHEN PROLONGING THE PREGNANCY IS HAZARDOUS -- eg PLACENTAL ABRUPTION, INTRAUTERINE INFECTION |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | preterm-labour#3 | `baf23bda` |  | NIFEDIPINE IS PREFERRED: IT APPEARS MORE EFFECTIVE THAN SALBUTAMOL -- FEWER BIRTHS WITHIN 7 DAYS OF TREATMENT -- HAS FEWER MATERNAL ADVERSE EFFECTS, AND IS ASSOCIATED WITH REDUCED NEONATAL MORBIDITY. Unlike salbutamol it is GIVEN ORALLY and MAY BE CONSIDERED FOR MAINTENANCE TREATMENT -- ⚠️ HOWEVER SUCH TREATMENT IS CONTROVERSIAL AS EVIDENCE FOR EFFICACY IS LACKING |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | preterm-labour#5 | `c6f513b0` |  | ANTENATAL CORTICOSTEROIDS: GIVE A SINGLE COURSE TO WOMEN WHO ARE AT RISK OF PRETERM DELIVERY WITHIN 7 DAYS AND ARE AT 24-34 WEEKS GESTATION -- ⚠️ SEE LOCAL PROTOCOLS AS THEY MAY DIFFER |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | preterm-labour#7 | `d48abd4a` |  | ⚠️ REPEAT CORTICOSTEROID COURSES: refer to local protocols or seek specialist advice. ACCORDING TO A SYSTEMATIC REVIEW, REPEAT COURSES HAVE SOME SHORT-TERM BENEFITS -- REDUCED RDS AND MORBIDITY -- COMPARED WITH A SINGLE COURSE, ⚠️ BUT WEIGHT, HEAD CIRCUMFERENCE AND LENGTH ARE REDUCED AT BIRTH. FOLLOW-UP AT 2-3 YEARS OF AGE SUGGESTED NO HARMS, NOR ANY BENEFITS |
| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | preterm-labour#8 | `fbb02620` |  | MAGNESIUM SULFATE, GIVEN TO WOMEN AT RISK OF PRETERM BIRTH, IS NEUROPROTECTIVE AND REDUCES THE RISK OF CEREBRAL PALSY IN THE INFANT. ⚠️ GIVE TO WOMEN UNDER 30 WEEKS GESTATION WHO ARE LIKELY TO DELIVER WITHIN 24 HOURS. ⚠️ NOTE THE THREE GESTATIONAL WINDOWS DIFFER: TOCOLYSIS UP TO 34 WEEKS, CORTICOSTEROIDS AT 24-34 WEEKS, MAGNESIUM SULFATE UNDER 30 WEEKS |
|  | anti-infectives-general-principles#29 | `0bd6d245` | [Principles of appropriate antimicrobial prescribing](https://app.tg.org.au/viewTopic?etgAccess=true&guidelinePage=Antibiotic&topicfile=bartonella-infections&guidelinename=auto&sectionId=c_ABG_Principles-of-appropriate-antimicrobial-prescribing_topic_2) | Switch IV to oral once clinically stable with functioning gut, improving fever, no unexplained haemodynamic instability and a suitable oral agent (not for meningitis-type infections); review empirical therapy daily in hospital. |

| ~ owner 2026-09-24 | benign-prostatic-hyperplasia-and-prostatitis#56 | `c609573f` |  | For chronic prostatitis or chronic pelvic pain syndrome AMH suggests a trial of an antibacterial as for chronic bacterial prostatitis may be worthwhile especially in recently diagnosed patients as there may be undiagnosed bacterial infection, that adequate analgesia such as an NSAID and relief of constipation may help, that one of the selective alpha-blockers may ease pain and improve urinary symptoms, and that quercetin 500 mg twice daily may relieve symptoms particularly pain based on small placebo-controlled studies |

AERUGINOSA COVER -- or ciprofloxacin. Start IV then change to oral when possible, USE CEFTRIAXONE IF GENTAMICIN IS CONTRAINDICATED OR IF IV TREATMENT WILL CONTINUE MORE THAN 72 HOURS, and usually treat for 7 to 10 days |

IN WOMEN, POSTCOITAL SINGLE-DOSE trimethoprim, cefalexin or nitrofurantoin may be an alternative to long-term prophylaxis |
